# Built-in rewrite rule inventory

<!-- Generated from Clockwork.Instrumentation.Rules.BuiltIn.RuleInventoryDocument.Render().
     Do not edit by hand; a test verifies this file matches the shipped rule set. -->

This is the exact, exhaustive surface the built-in rule sets redirect. Every other API is **not** rewritten. Instrumented closure binaries are simulation/test artifacts: every Controlled entry point requires an active Clockwork simulation, and an active simulation with no registered runtime service fails explicitly rather than use real time, randomness, or an uncontrolled task. Uninstrumented production binaries retain ordinary BCL behavior.

# Deterministic BCL rule set

Rule set id: `clockwork.bcl.deterministic`
Version: `3.0.0`
Supported runtime: `11.0`
Shim assembly: `Clockwork`

## Clock family

Policy: **Controlled**. Wall-clock, offset-clock, monotonic timestamp, and tick-counter reads dispatch to the node's simulated clock. Local-time APIs honour the configured simulation time zone; tick counters wrap with correct `int`/`long` semantics.

| Rule id | BCL target | Shim | Policy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `clockwork.bcl.datetime.now` | `System.DateTime::get_Now()` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.ControlledDateTime::GetNow()` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.bcl.datetime.utcnow` | `System.DateTime::get_UtcNow()` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.ControlledDateTime::GetUtcNow()` | Controlled |

## Identity family

Policy: **Controlled**. GUIDs draw deterministic bytes while preserving RFC 4122 variant and version. `CreateVersion7` encodes the simulated UTC millisecond timestamp in the first 48 bits; repeated calls at the same simulated instant share that timestamp (no monotonicity guarantee beyond the BCL contract).

| Rule id | BCL target | Shim | Policy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `clockwork.bcl.guid.newguid` | `System.Guid::NewGuid()` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.ControlledGuid::NewGuid()` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.bcl.guid.createversion7` | `System.Guid::CreateVersion7()` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.ControlledGuid::CreateVersion7()` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.bcl.guid.createversion7.timestamp` | `System.Guid::CreateVersion7(System.DateTimeOffset)` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.ControlledGuid::CreateVersion7(System.DateTimeOffset)` | Controlled |

## Random family

Policy: **Controlled**. `Random.Shared` and unseeded `new Random()` become per-node deterministic streams isolated from the scheduler/network/Buggify seed domains; explicitly seeded `new Random(int)` preserves the caller's seed exactly. Stable seed derivation uses `SimulationStableHash`. Every instance member, including the generic `NextInteger<T>` and `NextBinaryFloat<T>`, draws from the instance's stream.

| Rule id | BCL target | Shim | Policy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `clockwork.bcl.random.shared` | `System.Random::get_Shared()` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.ControlledRandom::GetShared()` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.bcl.random.ctor.unseeded` | `new System.Random()` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.ControlledRandom::CreateUnseeded()` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.bcl.random.ctor.seeded` | `new System.Random(System.Int32)` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.ControlledRandom::CreateSeeded(System.Int32)` | Controlled |

# Controlled task rule set

Rule set id: `clockwork.tasks.controlled`
Version: `3.0.0`
Supported runtime: `11.0`
Shim assembly: `Clockwork`

## TaskCombinators family

Policy: **Controlled**. `Task.WhenAll`/`WhenAny` (the non-generic `Task[]`, `IEnumerable<Task>`, .NET 9+ params `ReadOnlySpan<Task>`, and two-argument overloads, plus their generic `Task<TResult>` counterparts) and the `TaskExtensions.Unwrap` extension methods redirect to controlled combinators which register nothing on their antecedents. A result whose antecedents have already completed is computed by the BCL synchronously; otherwise the coordinator polls the antecedents and a controlled operation completes the result, with the BCL's argument validation, result, fault aggregation, and cancellation. `WhenAny` returns the first antecedent, in argument order, among those observed complete; the non-generic `WhenAll` aggregates faults in the order its antecedents are observed complete, with antecedents observed in the same scheduling step in argument order. `Task.WhenEach` (array, span, and enumerable, generic and non-generic) yields the tasks in the order the coordinator observes them complete, from the call on, through an enumerator whose `MoveNextAsync` a controlled operation completes, with the BCL's validation, single enumeration, and cancellation.

## TaskSynchronization family

Policy: **Controlled**. Every `Task.Wait`, `Task.WaitAll`, and `Task.WaitAny` overload redirects to a controlled wait that pumps the deterministic loop rather than blocking a physical thread, with timeouts that elapse in virtual time and the BCL's results and exceptions; a never-satisfiable wait surfaces as a precise deadlock diagnostic instead of hanging the scheduler. `TaskAsyncEnumerableExtensions.ToBlockingEnumerable` redirects to an enumerable that pumps the loop until each `MoveNextAsync` and the final `DisposeAsync` complete, with the BCL's token, early-exit disposal, and exception semantics.

## TaskContinuations family

Policy: **Controlled**. All 40 .NET 11 `Task.ContinueWith` and `Task<T>.ContinueWith` overloads (with and without state, a `CancellationToken`, `TaskContinuationOptions`, and a `TaskScheduler`) redirect. The result is a genuine BCL continuation task of a private gate, so its status, options, state, cancellation (including `LazyCancellation`), `NotOn*`/`OnlyOn*` filtering, and exceptions are the BCL's. The coordinator observes the antecedent complete and a controlled operation completes the gate, running the continuation inline on the logical thread (`ExecuteSynchronously` is a hint). The overloads without a scheduler use `TaskScheduler.Default`; an explicit `TaskScheduler.Default` or the simulation's own `SimulationTaskScheduler` is accepted. `LongRunning`, `AttachedToParent`, and any other scheduler cannot be preserved and are rejected.

| Rule id | BCL target | Shim | Policy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

## TaskCompletionSource family

Policy: **Controlled**. Rewritten code keeps the real `TaskCompletionSource` and `TaskCompletionSource<TResult>`, with their real creation options. The completion members (`SetResult`, `SetException`, `SetCanceled`, `SetFromTask`, and their `TrySet*` forms) redirect to shims which complete the source through the same BCL member and publish the race-detection release edge an await, wait, combinator, or continuation acquires. Outside a simulation (in a finalizer, or a callback from uninstrumented code) the shims call the BCL member alone. The constructors which take creation options redirect to factories which reject `AttachedToParent` inside a simulation, as the `Task` constructors do: Clockwork's controlled tasks do not model parent and child tasks.

| Rule id | BCL target | Shim | Policy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `clockwork.tasks.completionsource.setresult` | `System.Threading.Tasks.TaskCompletionSource::SetResult()` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.Tasks.ControlledTaskCompletionSource::SetResult(System.Threading.Tasks.TaskCompletionSource)` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.tasks.completionsource.trysetresult` | `System.Threading.Tasks.TaskCompletionSource::TrySetResult()` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.Tasks.ControlledTaskCompletionSource::TrySetResult(System.Threading.Tasks.TaskCompletionSource)` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.tasks.completionsource.setexception` | `System.Threading.Tasks.TaskCompletionSource::SetException(System.Exception)` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.Tasks.ControlledTaskCompletionSource::SetException(System.Threading.Tasks.TaskCompletionSource,System.Exception)` | Controlled |

## TaskTime family

## TaskScheduling family

Policy: **Controlled**. `Task.Run` (all `Action`/`Func<TResult>`/`Func<Task>`/`Func<Task<TResult>>` overloads, with and without a `CancellationToken`) redirects thread-pool scheduling into controlled operations. Each overload redirects to a controlled equivalent that schedules the delegate as a controlled operation on the simulation coordinator, preserving cancellation and unwrap semantics. `Task.Start` and `Task.RunSynchronously` run a task constructed with `new Task(...)` on the coordinator's `TaskScheduler`, with the BCL's validation and status transitions; a custom scheduler is rejected.

## AsyncMachinery family

Policy: **Controlled**. The compiler-generated builder and awaiter types of an `async` state machine (`AsyncTaskMethodBuilder`, `TaskAwaiter`, `ConfiguredTaskAwaitable`/`YieldAwaitable` and their awaiters, generic and non-generic) are substituted onto Clockwork's controlled equivalents by the member-aware pass, and `Task.Yield()` redirects to the controlled yield. Every awaited continuation is scheduled through the simulation coordinator instead of the thread pool, and `ConfigureAwait(false)` stays controlled. `AsyncHelpers.HandleAsyncEntryPoint`, through which an async `Main` returns, waits for its task through the controlled awaiter. A method compiled with runtime async has no state machine and resumes through the runtime's own continuation machinery, so rewriting one fails with `CWR0013`; the runtime-async `AsyncHelpers.Await` intrinsics taking a configured task awaitable are rejected.

## ValueTaskMachinery family

Policy: **Controlled**. The compiler-generated builder and awaiter types of an `async ValueTask`/`async ValueTask<T>` state machine (`AsyncValueTaskMethodBuilder`, `ValueTaskAwaiter`, `ConfiguredValueTaskAwaitable` and their awaiters, generic and non-generic) are substituted onto Clockwork's controlled equivalents by the member-aware pass, so every awaited value-task continuation is scheduled through the simulation coordinator. `ConfigureAwait(false)` stays controlled. The configured async disposable and enumerable that `await using` and `await foreach` consume (`ConfiguredAsyncDisposable`, `ConfiguredCancelableAsyncEnumerable<T>` and its `Enumerator`) are substituted with them, and the `TaskAsyncEnumerableExtensions.ConfigureAwait`/`WithCancellation` extensions redirect to factories for the controlled types, so iteration and dispose awaits resume through the coordinator and the configured token reaches `GetAsyncEnumerator` as in the BCL. The runtime-async `AsyncHelpers.Await` intrinsics taking a configured value-task awaitable are rejected. Synchronous blocking on a value task is not rewritten (a value task may be consumed only once); `await` is the supported controlled path.

## ValueTaskConversions family

Policy: **Controlled**. `ValueTask.AsTask`, `ValueTask<T>.AsTask`, `ValueTask.Preserve`, and `ValueTask<T>.Preserve` redirect. A value task backed by a pending `IValueTaskSource` is polled through the source's status by the coordinator, with no completion callback registered on the source, and a controlled operation consumes the completed source once through the BCL's own conversion. Any other value task is converted by the BCL itself, synchronously.

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `clockwork.tasks.valuetask.astask` | `System.Threading.Tasks.ValueTask::AsTask()` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.Tasks.ControlledValueTask::AsTask(System.Threading.Tasks.ValueTask&)` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.tasks.valuetask.preserve` | `System.Threading.Tasks.ValueTask::Preserve()` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.Tasks.ControlledValueTask::Preserve(System.Threading.Tasks.ValueTask&)` | Controlled |

## TaskFactory family

Policy: **Controlled**. All 24 .NET 11 `TaskFactory.StartNew` and `TaskFactory<T>.StartNew` overloads are classified, including state-carrying delegates and the full cancellation/options/scheduler forms. Each redirects to a controlled equivalent that schedules the delegate as a fresh logical strand while preserving state, cancellation, and results. Non-default schedulers and creation options whose semantics cannot be preserved are rejected precisely.

## TaskFactoryContinuations family

Policy: **Controlled**. All 48 .NET 11 `TaskFactory` and `TaskFactory<T>` `ContinueWhenAll` and `ContinueWhenAny` overloads redirect. The result is a genuine BCL continuation task of a private gate, with the BCL's argument validation, creation options, state (the continuation delegate), and cancellation, including a task canceled at creation for a canceled token. The coordinator observes the antecedents complete and a controlled operation completes the gate, running the continuation inline on the logical thread with a defensive copy of the antecedents, or with the first antecedent, in argument order, among those observed complete. The overloads without a scheduler use the factory's or `TaskScheduler.Default`; `LongRunning`, `AttachedToParent`, and schedulers other than `TaskScheduler.Default` and the simulation's own are rejected, as for `ContinueWith`.

## TaskFactoryFromAsync family

Policy: **Rejected**. Every `TaskFactory.FromAsync` and `TaskFactory<T>.FromAsync` overload is rejected at the call site: the task completes when an `IAsyncResult` does, on a thread of its implementation's choosing, outside Clockwork's control. Await a task-returning API instead.

| Rule id | BCL target | Shim | Policy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `clockwork.tasks.factory.fromasync` | `System.Threading.Tasks.TaskFactory::FromAsync(*)` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.Tasks.ControlledTaskFactory::RejectFromAsync(System.String)` | Rejected |
| `clockwork.tasks.factory.generic.fromasync` | `` System.Threading.Tasks.TaskFactory`1::FromAsync(*) `` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.Tasks.ControlledTaskFactory::RejectFromAsync(System.String)` | Rejected |

## Thread family

Policy: **Controlled**. `Thread` construction (`ThreadStart`/`ParameterizedThreadStart`, with and without a stack size), `Start`, `UnsafeStart` (which does not flow the `ExecutionContext`), `Join` (all overloads), `Sleep`, `Yield`, and `SpinWait` redirect to a controlled thread that maps each thread to a controlled operation on the simulation coordinator; `Join`/`Sleep` yield the logical thread via the deterministic loop rather than blocking a physical thread or consuming real time. OS-specific priority, apartment-state, and `Interrupt` operations cannot be modelled faithfully and are rejected with a precise diagnostic.

## ThreadPool family

Policy: **Controlled**. `ThreadPool.QueueUserWorkItem` (the `WaitCallback`, `WaitCallback`+state, and generic `Action<TState>`+state+preferLocal forms) and `UnsafeQueueUserWorkItem` (the `WaitCallback`+state, `IThreadPoolWorkItem`, and generic forms) queue the callback as a controlled operation on the simulation coordinator; the safe variants flow `ExecutionContext` while the unsafe variants do not, matching the BCL. The registered-wait APIs (`RegisterWaitForSingleObject`/`UnsafeRegisterWaitForSingleObject`, across the `uint`/`int`/`long`/`TimeSpan` timeout overloads) run as passive, event-driven controlled waits on the target handle's modelled signalled state: the callback fires with `timedOut: false` on a signal (an auto-reset handle consumes exactly one) or `timedOut: true` on the virtual-time deadline, honouring `executeOnlyOnce`, re-arming otherwise, and flowing `ExecutionContext` for the safe family only; the returned `RegisteredWaitHandle` is substituted with the controlled handle so `Unregister` stops the wait and signals its completion event. `UnsafeQueueNativeOverlapped` depends on native I/O and is rejected with a precise diagnostic.

| `clockwork.threadpool.queue.waitcallback.state` | `System.Threading.ThreadPool::QueueUserWorkItem(System.Threading.WaitCallback,System.Object)` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.ControlledThreadPool::QueueUserWorkItem(System.Threading.WaitCallback,System.Object)` | Controlled |

## Timers family

Policy: **Controlled**. `System.Threading.Timer`, `System.Timers.Timer`, and `PeriodicTimer` are substituted with controlled virtual-time implementations. `TimeProvider.System` and `CreateTimer` bridge to the same scheduler; unsupported provider and designer marshaling paths reject precisely.

| Rule id | BCL target | Shim | Policy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `clockwork.timer.threading.type` | `System.Threading.Timer` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.ControlledTimer` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.timer.component.type` | `System.Timers.Timer` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Timers.ControlledTimer` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.timer.periodic.type` | `System.Threading.PeriodicTimer` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.ControlledPeriodicTimer` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.timeprovider.system` | `System.TimeProvider::get_System()` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.ControlledTimeProvider::get_System()` | Controlled |
| `clockwork.timeprovider.createtimer` | `System.TimeProvider::CreateTimer(System.Threading.TimerCallback,System.Object,System.TimeSpan,System.TimeSpan)` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.ControlledTimeProvider::CreateTimer(System.TimeProvider,System.Threading.TimerCallback,System.Object,System.TimeSpan,System.TimeSpan)` | Controlled |

## CancellationTimers family

Policy: **Controlled**. `CancellationTokenSource` timed constructors and `CancelAfter` use resettable virtual deadlines. Manual cancellation, reset, and disposal remove stale registrations before they can fire.

## Parallel family

Policy: **Controlled**. `Parallel.ForEachAsync`, `Parallel.ForAsync`, `Parallel.Invoke`, `Parallel.For`, and `Parallel.ForEach` run on a deterministic pool of controlled workers on the simulation coordinator, and the overloads whose body receives a `ParallelLoopState` are rejected. `Environment.ProcessorCount` and the range factories `Partitioner.Create(fromInclusive, toExclusive)` use the simulation's processor count, which sizes the loops without a limit. [Parallel loops](compatibility.md#parallel-loops) describes their behavior.

## Channels family

Policy: **Controlled**. `ChannelReader<T>.ReadAllAsync` and the base `ChannelReader<T>.ReadAsync` and `ChannelWriter<T>.WriteAsync`, which the BCL implements as async methods in the channels assembly whose awaits resume on the thread pool, call a reader's or writer's override as written and otherwise run the BCL's algorithm with controlled awaits on the coordinator. The members left as BCL calls complete through the channel's own state, which controlled code changes on the logical thread.

## Plinq family

Policy: **Rejected**. Every `ParallelEnumerable.AsParallel` overload, and `ParallelEnumerable.Range`, `Repeat`, and `Empty`, are rejected at the call site with a precise diagnostic: PLINQ runs a query on worker tasks of `TaskScheduler.Default` and blocks the calling thread on framework waits until they finish. These members are the only public way to create a `ParallelQuery<T>`, so the operators over one stay BCL calls.

## BlockingCollection family

Policy: **Controlled**. The `BlockingCollection<T>` members which add, take, or complete adding (`Add`, `TryAdd`, `Take`, `TryTake`, `GetConsumingEnumerable`, `CompleteAdding`, and the static `AddToAny`, `TryAddToAny`, `TakeFromAny`, and `TryTakeFromAny`) redirect to controlled shims over the real collection. Each makes the BCL's own zero-timeout attempt, which validates, adds, or takes as the BCL does, and when that would have waited on the framework's semaphores, parks the caller on the coordinator until the collection can satisfy it, adding is completed (a waiting adder then throws and a waiting taker returns `false`, as in the BCL), the token is cancelled, or the timeout elapses in virtual time. A wait which nothing can satisfy is reported as a deadlock. Adds and `CompleteAdding` publish a race-detection edge which takes acquire.

## AsyncIO family

Policy: **Controlled**. The asynchronous members of `Stream`, `TextReader`, and `TextWriter` whose base implementations run the synchronous member on the thread pool (`ReadAsync`, `WriteAsync`, `FlushAsync`, `ReadLineAsync`, `ReadToEndAsync`, `ReadBlockAsync`, `WriteLineAsync`), block the caller on a semaphore or task (`Stream.BeginRead`/`EndRead`/`BeginWrite`/`EndWrite`), or await the other members through BCL awaits (`Stream.CopyToAsync`) call an override as written and otherwise run the BCL's base algorithm on the coordinator: each synchronous read, write, or flush runs as a fresh controlled operation concurrent with the caller, a stream's base reads and writes are serialized in FIFO order as the BCL's semaphore serializes them, and every await is controlled. An override's base call runs the base algorithm. The non-virtual members (`Stream.ReadExactlyAsync`, `ReadAtLeastAsync`, and the overloads which forward to a virtual member) run the BCL's algorithm over the controlled virtual members. `DisposeAsync` and the synchronous members run on the calling thread and stay BCL calls.

## BackgroundWork family

Policy: **Rejected**. `BackgroundWorker.RunWorkerAsync` and `BackgroundService.StartAsync` are rejected at the call site with a precise diagnostic: they start the application's `DoWork` handler or `ExecuteAsync` on the thread pool (`Task.Factory.StartNew` on `TaskScheduler.Default`, and the real `Task.Run`), and each type raises or stores that work through private state, so no shim can run it on the coordinator with the BCL's semantics. A call through `IHostedService`, as a host makes, names the interface rather than `BackgroundService.StartAsync`, so it stays a BCL call.

| Rule id | BCL target | Shim | Policy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `clockwork.backgroundworker.runworkerasync` | `System.ComponentModel.BackgroundWorker::RunWorkerAsync(*)` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.ComponentModel.ControlledBackgroundWorker::RejectUnsupported(System.String)` | Rejected |
| `clockwork.backgroundservice.startasync` | `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting.BackgroundService::StartAsync(System.Threading.CancellationToken)` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting.ControlledBackgroundService::RejectUnsupported(System.String)` | Rejected |

## Dataflow family

Policy: **Rejected**. Every TPL Dataflow block constructor, and the `DataflowBlock` helpers which schedule work or wait (`Choose`, `Receive`, `ReceiveAsync`, `ReceiveAllAsync`, `SendAsync`, `OutputAvailableAsync`), are rejected at the call site with a precise diagnostic. A block runs its delegates on its options' `TaskScheduler`, but wires cancellation and completion propagation through continuations on `TaskScheduler.Default` whatever that scheduler is, and the helpers time out on real timers, so a scheduler substitution would not keep its work on the coordinator. Use `System.Threading.Channels` with `Task.Run` or `Parallel.ForEachAsync` instead.

## WebSockets family

Policy: **Controlled**. `WebSocket.CreateFromStream` and `WebSocket.CreateClientWebSocket` are rejected at the call site with a precise diagnostic: the framework's WebSocket protocol over a caller's stream sends keep-alive pings from a real timer on the thread pool and times out its closing handshake in real time. The `WebSocketStream.Create` overloads which may make the stream own its socket are controlled: a stream which owns its socket bounds the close on disposal with a real timer, so they throw `SimulationApiException` for a finite, non-zero close timeout (an owning stream's default is 16 seconds), and otherwise return the BCL's stream.

## HttpClient family

Policy: **Controlled**. Every `HttpClient` member which sends a request, `HttpMessageInvoker.Send` and `SendAsync`, and every `HttpClientJsonExtensions` member redirect to shims which throw `SimulationApiException` when the client's `Timeout` is finite, as it is by default (100 seconds), and otherwise call the BCL member: the BCL bounds each request, and the JSON extensions the reading of its response, with the timeout on a real timer. Set `Timeout` to `Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` and bound a request with a token which a virtual delay cancels. A call through `HttpMessageInvoker` is checked when its receiver is a client; the virtual members' base calls run the base implementation.

## Hosting family

Policy: **Controlled**. Every `HostingAbstractionsHostExtensions` member is controlled. The BCL's members await the host's work in framework code, whose continuations leave the logical thread where the simulation's synchronization context is current; block the caller on it (`Start`, `Run`, `WaitForShutdown`); or bound a stop with a real timer (`StopAsync(IHost, TimeSpan)`). The shims run the BCL's steps in the same order, awaiting through the coordinator, waiting with the controlled wait, which pumps the coordinator so the host's work runs while the caller waits, and stopping with a token which a virtual timer cancels once the timeout elapses in simulated time.

## Monitor family

Policy: **Controlled**. The complete .NET 11 `Monitor` surface is classified: synchronization and C# `lock (object)` lowering are controlled with deterministic virtual-time deadlines, while the process-wide `LockContentionCount` metric is rejected because it has no per-simulation meaning.

## Semaphore family

Policy: **Controlled**. The .NET 11 `SemaphoreSlim` constructors, counts, waits, releases, and disposal are controlled; `AvailableWaitHandle` returns a controlled manual-reset bridge whose signal tracks whether the permit count is positive and which composes with the controlled wait-handle surface.

## Interlocked family

Policy: **Controlled**. The full .NET 11 `Interlocked` surface - `Increment`/`Decrement`/`Add` (`int`/`long`/`uint`/`ulong`), `And`/`Or` (`int`/`long`/`uint`/`ulong` and the generic integer or enum overload), `Exchange`/`CompareExchange` (every primitive, native-int, floating-point, reference, and generic overload), `Read` (`long`/`ulong`), and the memory barriers - redirects each call site to a shim with the identical `ref`-first signature. Clockwork's cooperative single-logical-thread scheduler makes every read-modify-write an indivisible step (never split, never interleaved mid-operation), so the shim delegates to the real primitive and preserves exact atomic return, overflow, and reference-write semantics under the active simulation. The exploration policy injects no mid-operation scheduling point; the single delegation site is the race-exploration access-tracking attachment point.

| `clockwork.interlocked.increment.int32` | `System.Threading.Interlocked::Increment(System.Int32&)` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.ControlledInterlocked::Increment(System.Int32&)` | Controlled |

## Volatile family

Policy: **Controlled**. The full .NET 11 `Volatile` surface - `Read`/`Write` (every primitive, native-int, floating-point, and generic reference overload) and the `ReadBarrier`/`WriteBarrier` fences - redirects each call site to a shim with the identical `ref`-first signature. Under the cooperative single-logical-thread scheduler a volatile access is an indivisible step, so the shim delegates to the real primitive and preserves the exact value read/written together with the acquire (read) / release (write) fence intent. The single delegation site is the race-exploration access-tracking attachment point.

## SpinWait family

Policy: **Controlled**. `System.Threading.SpinWait` is a value type retargeted by whole-type substitution (like `System.Threading.Lock`): every local/field/parameter typed `SpinWait`, each `new SpinWait()`/`default`, the instance members (`Count`, `NextSpinWillYield`, `Reset`, both `SpinOnce` overloads) and the static `SpinUntil` overloads remap onto the controlled struct. Inside a simulation a spin never burns CPU or consumes real time: `SpinOnce` is a cooperative no-op that only advances the observable spin count, and `SpinUntil` pumps the deterministic loop until its predicate holds (a never-satisfiable predicate surfaces as the loop-model deadlock diagnostic). The finite `SpinUntil` overloads use a first-winner virtual-time deadline.

| Rule id | BCL target | Shim | Policy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `clockwork.spinwait.type` | `System.Threading.SpinWait` | `Clockwork!Clockwork.Shims.System.Threading.ControlledSpinWait` | Controlled |

## WaitHandle family

Policy: **Controlled**. The controlled event / wait-handle surface - `AutoResetEvent`, `ManualResetEvent`, `EventWaitHandle`, and the shared `WaitHandle` operations. Each concrete event is a sealed BCL class, so the real object is retained as an identity handle while its signaled state and a deterministic FIFO waiter set live in a side table; every `new` redirects to a `Create` factory and each instance member is a receiver-first shim. `WaitOne` (all five overloads) pumps the deterministic loop until the event is signaled - a never-satisfiable wait surfaces as the loop-model deadlock diagnostic rather than hanging - and `Set`/`Reset` model exact reset-mode semantics: an auto-reset `Set` wakes and consumes exactly one eligible waiter (or leaves the event signaled until the next `WaitOne` consumes it), while a manual-reset `Set` releases every waiter and stays signaled until `Reset`. The static multi-handle operations `WaitAny` (returns the lowest-index signaled handle) and `WaitAll` (waits until every handle is simultaneously signaled, then consumes them atomically so an auto-reset handle is never partially consumed) register across all handles with no lost signals, validating null/empty/over-64 arrays and - for `WaitAll` - duplicate handles; `SignalAndWait` atomically signals the first handle then waits on the second. Finite timeouts use a first-winner virtual-time deadline (zero polls, infinite never times out); `Dispose`/`Close` mark the modelled state disposed. Named / cross-process APIs (named constructors, `OpenExisting`, `TryOpenExisting`) and the raw native-handle accessors (`Handle`, `SafeWaitHandle`) cannot be modelled in a single simulated process and are rejected with a precise diagnostic.

## Mutex family

Policy: **Controlled**. Unnamed `Mutex` construction and `ReleaseMutex` are controlled through the wait-handle kernel. Named constructors (including null-name forms that the shim conditionally treats as unnamed) and `OpenExisting`/`TryOpenExisting` are classified Rejected because a non-null name is cross-process kernel state. Ownership and recursion are logical-strand state; owner exit without `ReleaseMutex` leaves the mutex owned so a later indefinite wait reports the controlled deadlock diagnostic rather than simulating `AbandonedMutexException`.

## SynchronizationContext family

Policy: **Controlled**. `SynchronizationContext` ambient-context and callback-dispatch members redirect to controlled shims. `Post` queues through the coordinator and `Send` runs on the current logical strand; custom context dispatch is not invoked. Its raw native-handle `Wait` member is Rejected before it can block a physical thread.

## UncontrolledInvocation family

Policy: **Rejected**. Process control and abrupt-termination APIs (`Process.Start`/`Run`/`RunAsync`/`RunAndCaptureText`/`RunAndCaptureTextAsync`/`StartAndForget`, the `Start` instance/`Kill`/`Signal` members, the `WaitForExit`/`WaitForExitAsync`/`WaitForExitStatus`/`TryWaitForExitStatus`/`WaitForExitStatusAsync` waits, the `ReadAllBytes`/`ReadAllText`/`ReadAllLines` reads (and their async forms) that wait for the process to close its output, the `WaitForInputIdle` waits, the `Responding` getter, `CloseMainWindow`, the `EnableRaisingEvents` setter and `BeginOutputReadLine`/`BeginErrorReadLine` (which raise events on threads of the BCL's choosing as the process exits or writes), the `Process` priority, processor-affinity, and working-set setters and the `ProcessThread` scheduling setters, the `SafeProcessHandle` `Start`/`Signal`/`Resume`/`Kill` and wait members, the `WindowsProcessStartArguments`/`UnixProcessStartArguments` `Start` launchers, and `Environment.Exit`/`FailFast`) cannot be modelled inside a single simulated process at all. A throwing guard is injected before each call site so a rewritten assembly can never launch, signal, wait on, reschedule, or terminate a real OS process; unlike the controlled shims the rejection is unconditional (it fires whether or not a simulation is active).

# Race exploration instrumentation inventory

This inventory is enabled only when instrumentation mode is `RaceExploration`; `Controlled` mode injects none of these calls.

| Surface | Instrumentation | Tracked identity |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ldfld` / `stfld` on reference types | Read/write scheduling point | Weak object identity + field member |
| `ldsfld` / `stsfld` | Read/write scheduling point | Static field member |
| volatile field access | Schedule-only point before the `volatile.` prefix | Not race-tracked |
| `ldelem.*` / `stelem.*` vector arrays | Read/write scheduling point | Weak array identity + element index |
| field-address, indirect/object, and `ldelema` access | Schedule-only point | Not race-tracked |
| `brtrue` / `brfalse` | Control-flow scheduling point | n/a |
| `List<T>`, `Dictionary<TKey,TValue>`, `HashSet<T>` direct concrete members | Read/write/iteration point after the original call | Weak collection identity |
| `ConcurrentBag<T>`, `ConcurrentDictionary<TKey,TValue>`, `ConcurrentQueue<T>`, `ConcurrentStack<T>` direct concrete members | Interleaving point after the original call | Not reported as a race |

Limits: constructors and property accessors are excluded; generated `MoveNext` methods are visited, with generated value-type state fields schedule-only. Multidimensional arrays, interface-typed and tail-prefixed collection calls, reflection/dynamic dispatch, spans, unmanaged memory, and arbitrary pointer offsets are not assigned tracked locations.

# Members left as BCL calls

Every public and protected member of a type the built-in rules target is controlled or rejected by a rule above, or is listed below with the reason a call to it stays a BCL call. A type is targeted when a rule names or substitutes it, or when a member's signature mentions a class, struct, or interface of `System.Diagnostics.Process.dll`. A test classifies each targeted type against the reference assemblies of the supported runtime, so a member a new runtime adds fails that test until it is classified. Snapshots of the reference pack's assemblies and of the public types in the namespaces of the targeted types fail when a runtime adds or removes one.

| Reason | Behavior | Documented hole |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Value` | Computes its result from the receiver and its arguments, or creates, configures, or releases the receiver. | No |
| `CultureOrTimeZone` | Uses or sets the current culture, or converts local time with the host's time zone, so its result follows the host's configuration. | Yes |
| `HostState` | Reads or changes state of the host process or machine (environment variables, paths, identity, privileges, and processor, memory, and thread-pool statistics), so a simulation observes the host's values. | Yes |
| `Process` | Looks up, configures, reads, or releases an OS process or its handle, so a simulation observes the live process. The members that start, signal, wait on, reschedule, or terminate a process are rejected (`UncontrolledInvocation` family). | Yes |
| `Dispatch` | Runs on the receiver. `new Random()`, `Random.Shared`, `RandomNumberGenerator.Create`, and `TimeProvider.System` return instances that draw from the simulation's seeded streams and virtual clock. | No |
| `RealClock` | Reads the host's monotonic clock. `Stopwatch.GetTimestamp()` and `Stopwatch.GetElapsedTime(long)` are controlled. | Yes |
| `ProcessWideId` | Returns an identifier drawn from a process-wide counter, so its value depends on the tasks and threads the process created before. | Yes |
| `CurrentThread` | Reads or changes the calling thread's state (its `Thread` object, thread-local data slots, principal, and affinity hints), which belongs to the physical thread that runs every logical thread or flows with the `ExecutionContext` as in the BCL. | Yes |
| `IdentityHandle` | Reads the BCL `Thread` object that a controlled thread keeps as its identity. That object never runs, so a controlled thread reports itself unstarted. | Yes |
| `ConstructorCall` | A rule rewrites `new` of the type, but a constructor `call` to this one (a derived type's constructor chaining to it, or a value type constructed in place) runs the BCL constructor, so the instance keeps BCL state, such as an unseeded generator, a real timer, or a named kernel object. | Yes |
| `Fence` | Performs one memory access or fence, which the cooperative scheduler runs as an indivisible step. Race exploration does not track it. | No |
| `Blocks` | Blocks the calling physical thread as in the BCL, which stalls the simulation until it returns. Use `await` or a controlled overload. | Yes |
| `Schedules` | Runs its work through a BCL task scheduler, the thread pool, or a new physical thread rather than the simulation's coordinator. | Yes |
| `RuntimeAsync` | Runtime-async intrinsic, which suspends its caller through the runtime's own continuation machinery. Rewriting a method compiled with runtime async fails with `CWR0013`. | No |
| `AlwaysThrows` | Throws on .NET 11: `PlatformNotSupportedException`, or `InvalidOperationException` for `GetCompressedStack` and `SetCompressedStack`. | No |
| `SubstitutedTypeProtected` | Protected member of a substituted type, which only a derived type can call. Rewriting a type derived from a substituted type fails. | No |
| `Channel` | Reads or changes the channel's state on the calling thread, or returns a waiter which a later change to that state completes. Controlled code makes those changes on the logical thread and awaits the waiter through a controlled awaiter, so the member keeps its BCL behavior. | No |
| `ParallelQuery` | Operates on a PLINQ `ParallelQuery<T>`, which only the rejected `AsParallel`, `Range`, `Repeat`, and `Empty` create (`Plinq` family). A query that uninstrumented code creates and passes to rewritten code runs on worker tasks of `TaskScheduler.Default`, as in the BCL. | Yes |
| `SynchronousIO` | Reads, writes, flushes, or disposes on the calling thread, which is the logical thread: a synchronous I/O member, or `DisposeAsync`, whose base implementation calls `Dispose`. The asynchronous members whose base implementations run these on the thread pool are controlled (`AsyncIO` family). | No |
| `FrameworkWrapper` | Returns a framework wrapper over the given instances (`Stream.Synchronized`, `TextReader.Synchronized`, `TextWriter.Synchronized`, `TextWriter.CreateBroadcasting`). The wrapper's own members are classified with its type, but its calls to a wrapped instance are framework calls, so they reach that instance's base asynchronous members as in the BCL: `Stream.Synchronized` reads and writes through the wrapped stream's `BeginRead`/`BeginWrite`, and a broadcasting writer awaits each writer's asynchronous members. | Yes |
| `DataflowBlock` | Operates on a TPL Dataflow block, which rewritten code cannot create: every block constructor is rejected (`Dataflow` family). A block which uninstrumented code creates and passes to rewritten code runs its work on its options' `TaskScheduler` and its completion propagation on `TaskScheduler.Default`, as in the BCL. | Yes |
| `BackgroundWork` | Configures a `BackgroundWorker` or `BackgroundService`, raises its events on the caller, or observes or stops work which only the rejected `RunWorkerAsync` and `StartAsync` begin (`BackgroundWork` family). Work which a call through `IHostedService` starts, as a host makes, runs on the thread pool, where the thread-affinity check catches its first controlled call. | Yes |
| `WebSocket` | Runs on the receiving `WebSocket`, or on the `WebSocket` a `WebSocketStream` wraps: an application's own implementation, a `ClientWebSocket` over an OS connection, or one uninstrumented code creates. The framework's implementation over a caller's stream is created only by the rejected `CreateFromStream` and `CreateClientWebSocket` (`WebSockets` family). | Yes |

# Concurrency entry points: status

A test scans every managed implementation assembly of the shared framework the tests run on for the public members, and protected members of unsealed types, of the reference pack which reach a concurrency escape: thread-pool queueing, `TaskScheduler.Default` or a member which schedules onto it (`Task.Run`, `Task.Start`, `TaskFactory.StartNew`), `Thread.Start`, a real timer, or an async method. It follows calls within one assembly, up to 4 deep, not counting a call to another overload or to a local function or lambda compiled from the caller's body. Every member it finds is controlled or rejected by a rule, listed under [Members left as BCL calls](#members-left-as-bcl-calls), listed under [Audited framework members](#audited-framework-members) with the reason it needs no rule, or declared by an assembly listed under [Audited framework assemblies](#audited-framework-assemblies), whose reason covers its members. The implementation assemblies differ by operating system, so the lists hold the candidates of Windows, Linux, and macOS. A runtime which adds or removes such a member fails the test until it is classified; the members classified by their assembly are kept in a snapshot, so one a runtime adds to such an assembly fails until it is reviewed against the assembly's reason.

| Entry points | Status under simulation |
| --- | --- |
| `Task.Run`, `Task.Start`, `Task.RunSynchronously`, `new Task`, `new Task<T>`, `Task.CreationOptions` | Controlled ([`TaskScheduling` family](#taskscheduling-family)). |
| `TaskFactory.StartNew`, `TaskFactory<T>.StartNew` | Controlled ([`TaskFactory` family](#taskfactory-family)). |
| `Task.ContinueWith`, `Task<T>.ContinueWith` | Controlled ([`TaskContinuations` family](#taskcontinuations-family)). |
| `TaskFactory.ContinueWhenAll`, `TaskFactory.ContinueWhenAny`, `TaskFactory<T>.ContinueWhenAll`, `TaskFactory<T>.ContinueWhenAny` | Controlled ([`TaskFactoryContinuations` family](#taskfactorycontinuations-family)). |
| `TaskFactory.FromAsync`, `TaskFactory<T>.FromAsync` | Rejected ([`TaskFactoryFromAsync` family](#taskfactoryfromasync-family)). |
| `TaskCompletionSource.SetResult`, `TaskCompletionSource.TrySetResult`, `TaskCompletionSource.SetException`, `TaskCompletionSource.TrySetException`, `TaskCompletionSource.SetCanceled`, `TaskCompletionSource.TrySetCanceled`, `TaskCompletionSource.SetFromTask`, `TaskCompletionSource.TrySetFromTask`, `TaskCompletionSource<T>.SetResult`, `TaskCompletionSource<T>.TrySetResult`, `TaskCompletionSource<T>.SetException`, `TaskCompletionSource<T>.TrySetException`, `TaskCompletionSource<T>.SetCanceled`, `TaskCompletionSource<T>.TrySetCanceled`, `TaskCompletionSource<T>.SetFromTask`, `TaskCompletionSource<T>.TrySetFromTask`, `new TaskCompletionSource`, `new TaskCompletionSource<T>` | Controlled ([`TaskCompletionSource` family](#taskcompletionsource-family)). |
| `Task.WhenAll`, `Task.WhenAny`, `Task.WhenEach`, `TaskExtensions.Unwrap` | Controlled ([`TaskCombinators` family](#taskcombinators-family)). |
| `Task.Wait`, `Task.WaitAll`, `Task.WaitAny`, `Task<T>.Result`, `ValueTask<T>.Result`, `TaskAsyncEnumerableExtensions.ToBlockingEnumerable` | Controlled ([`TaskSynchronization` family](#tasksynchronization-family)). |
| `Task.Delay`, `Task.WaitAsync`, `Task<T>.WaitAsync` | Controlled ([`TaskTime` family](#tasktime-family)). |
| `AsyncTaskMethodBuilder`, `AsyncTaskMethodBuilder<T>`, `TaskAwaiter`, `TaskAwaiter<T>`, `ConfiguredTaskAwaitable`, `ConfiguredTaskAwaitable<T>`, `ConfiguredTaskAwaitable.ConfiguredTaskAwaiter`, `ConfiguredTaskAwaitable<T>.ConfiguredTaskAwaiter`, `YieldAwaitable`, `YieldAwaitable.YieldAwaiter`, `Task.Yield`, `AsyncHelpers.Await`, `AsyncHelpers.HandleAsyncEntryPoint` | Controlled; rejected: `AsyncHelpers.Await` ([`AsyncMachinery` family](#asyncmachinery-family)). |
| `AsyncValueTaskMethodBuilder`, `AsyncValueTaskMethodBuilder<T>`, `ValueTaskAwaiter`, `ValueTaskAwaiter<T>`, `ConfiguredValueTaskAwaitable`, `ConfiguredValueTaskAwaitable<T>`, `ConfiguredValueTaskAwaitable.ConfiguredValueTaskAwaiter`, `ConfiguredValueTaskAwaitable<T>.ConfiguredValueTaskAwaiter`, `AsyncHelpers.Await`, `ConfiguredAsyncDisposable`, `ConfiguredCancelableAsyncEnumerable<T>`, `ConfiguredCancelableAsyncEnumerable<T>.Enumerator`, `TaskAsyncEnumerableExtensions.ConfigureAwait`, `TaskAsyncEnumerableExtensions.WithCancellation` | Controlled; rejected: `AsyncHelpers.Await` ([`ValueTaskMachinery` family](#valuetaskmachinery-family)). |
| `ValueTask.AsTask`, `ValueTask.Preserve`, `ValueTask<T>.AsTask`, `ValueTask<T>.Preserve` | Controlled ([`ValueTaskConversions` family](#valuetaskconversions-family)). |
| `Environment.CurrentManagedThreadId`, `new Thread`, `Thread.Start`, `Thread.UnsafeStart`, `Thread.Join`, `Thread.Sleep`, `Thread.SpinWait`, `Thread.Yield`, `Thread.Priority`, `Thread.Interrupt`, `Thread.SetApartmentState`, `Thread.ApartmentState`, `Thread.TrySetApartmentState` | Controlled; rejected: `Thread.Priority`, `Thread.Interrupt`, `Thread.SetApartmentState`, `Thread.ApartmentState`, `Thread.TrySetApartmentState` ([`Thread` family](#thread-family)). |
| `ThreadPool.QueueUserWorkItem`, `ThreadPool.UnsafeQueueUserWorkItem`, `ThreadPool.UnsafeQueueNativeOverlapped`, `RegisteredWaitHandle`, `ThreadPool.RegisterWaitForSingleObject`, `ThreadPool.UnsafeRegisterWaitForSingleObject` | Controlled; rejected: `ThreadPool.UnsafeQueueNativeOverlapped` ([`ThreadPool` family](#threadpool-family)). |
| `Timer`, `PeriodicTimer`, `TimeProvider.System`, `TimeProvider.CreateTimer` | Controlled ([`Timers` family](#timers-family)). |
| `new CancellationTokenSource`, `CancellationTokenSource.CancelAfter`, `CancellationTokenSource.Cancel`, `CancellationTokenSource.CancelAsync`, `CancellationTokenSource.TryReset`, `CancellationTokenSource.Dispose` | Controlled ([`CancellationTimers` family](#cancellationtimers-family)). |
| `Parallel.Invoke`, `Parallel.For`, `Parallel.ForEach`, `Parallel.ForAsync`, `Parallel.ForEachAsync`, `Environment.ProcessorCount`, `Partitioner.Create` | Controlled; rejected: `Parallel.For` (some overloads), `Parallel.ForEach` (some overloads) ([`Parallel` family](#parallel-family)). |

Use `await` or a controlled overload. |
| `new ChannelReader<T>`, `ChannelReader<T>.CanCount`, `ChannelReader<T>.CanPeek`, `ChannelReader<T>.Completion`, `ChannelReader<T>.Count`, `ChannelReader<T>.TryPeek`, `ChannelReader<T>.TryRead`, `ChannelReader<T>.WaitToReadAsync`, `new ChannelWriter<T>`, `ChannelWriter<T>.Complete`, `ChannelWriter<T>.TryComplete`, `ChannelWriter<T>.TryWrite`, `ChannelWriter<T>.WaitToWriteAsync` | BCL call (`Channel`): Reads or changes the channel's state on the calling thread, or returns a waiter which a later change to that state completes. Controlled code makes those changes on the logical thread and awaits the waiter through a controlled awaiter, so the member keeps its BCL behavior. |
| `AsyncHelpers.UnsafeAwaitAwaiter`, `AsyncHelpers.AwaitAwaiter`, `AsyncHelpers.Await` | BCL call (`RuntimeAsync`): Runtime-async intrinsic, which suspends its caller through the runtime's own continuation machinery. Rewriting a method compiled with runtime async fails with `CWR0013`. |
| `BinaryWriter`, `BufferedStream`, `CancellationTokenRegistration`, `IndentedTextWriter`, `MemoryStream`, `ReadOnlyMemoryStream`, `StreamReader`, `StreamWriter`, `TaskToAsyncResult`, `WritableMemoryStream` ([members](#audited-framework-members)); the assemblies `Microsoft.Extensions.Caching.Abstractions`, `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting.Abstractions`, `Microsoft.Extensions.Options`, `System.ComponentModel.Annotations`, `System.Data.Common`, `System.Formats.Tar`, `System.IO.Compression`, `System.IO.Compression.Brotli`, `System.IO.Pipelines`, `System.Linq.AsyncEnumerable`, `System.Memory`, `System.Net.Http`, `System.Net.Http.Json`, `System.Net.Security`, `System.Net.ServerSentEvents`, `System.Net.WebSockets`, `System.Private.Xml`, `System.Private.Xml.Linq`, `System.Security.Cryptography`, `System.Text.Json` ([assemblies](#audited-framework-assemblies)) | `FrameworkAwait`: A framework async method or adapter. It runs on the caller until it awaits; its continuations follow the BCL's dispatch, running inline where controlled code completes the awaited operation, or on the thread pool when that operation runs its continuations asynchronously, where the thread-affinity check catches their first controlled call. |
| `File`, `FileStream`, `RandomAccess` ([members](#audited-framework-members)); the assemblies `System.IO.Compression.ZipFile`, `System.IO.Pipes`, `System.Net.HttpListener`, `System.Net.Mail`, `System.Net.NameResolution`, `System.Net.Ping`, `System.Net.Quic`, `System.Net.Requests`, `System.Net.Sockets`, `System.Net.WebClient`, `System.Net.WebSockets.Client` ([assemblies](#audited-framework-assemblies)) | `OsResource`: Performs I/O on an OS file, socket, or network connection, which completes on the operating system's I/O threads or the thread pool. A simulation performs I/O through the system under test's own abstractions, which it substitutes. |
| `DataflowBlockOptions`, `ExecutionDataflowBlockOptions`, `GroupingDataflowBlockOptions`, `ParallelOptions`, `TaskScheduler` ([members](#audited-framework-members)) | `SchedulerValue`: Reads or stores `TaskScheduler.Default` or the current scheduler without queueing work to it. The controlled APIs which take a scheduler accept `TaskScheduler.Default` and run the work on the coordinator. |
| `ConcurrentExclusiveSchedulerPair`, `TaskScheduler` ([members](#audited-framework-members)) | `CustomScheduler`: Belongs to a task scheduler other than the coordinator's, which queues to the thread pool. Every controlled API which takes a scheduler rejects it, so controlled work never reaches it. |
| `ManualResetValueTaskSourceCore<T>`, `PoolingAsyncValueTaskMethodBuilder`, `PoolingAsyncValueTaskMethodBuilder<T>` ([members](#audited-framework-members)) | `ValueTaskSource`: Completes, or registers a continuation on, an `IValueTaskSource`, whose continuations can run on the thread pool. Controlled awaits poll the source's status and register no continuation, so such a continuation comes only from uninstrumented code. |
| `AsyncIteratorMethodBuilder`, `AsyncVoidMethodBuilder`, `PoolingAsyncValueTaskMethodBuilder`, `PoolingAsyncValueTaskMethodBuilder<T>` ([members](#audited-framework-members)) | `UnhandledException`: Rethrows an exception on the thread pool when it escapes an `async void` method or an awaiter's `OnCompleted` throws, which ends the process, as in .NET. |
| `EventListener`, `MessageProcessingHandler`, `Progress<T>` ([members](#audited-framework-members)); the assemblies `System.IO.FileSystem.Watcher`, `System.Net.NetworkInformation`, `System.Transactions.Local` ([assemblies](#audited-framework-assemblies)) | `AffinityBackstop`: Runs a callback of the caller's on a thread of the BCL's choosing. The thread-affinity check fails the run at the callback's first controlled call; a callback which only computes or changes shared state runs unchecked. |
| `StringWriter` ([members](#audited-framework-members)) | `CompletesOnCaller`: Completes on the caller: the receiver's overrides of the members it awaits complete synchronously. |
| the assemblies `System.Text.RegularExpressions` ([assemblies](#audited-framework-assemblies)) | `WaitsForFrameworkWork`: Runs framework code, and none of the caller's, on another thread while the calling thread blocks until it finishes, so the call returns the BCL's result on the logical thread with no scheduling point. |
| Continuations which uninstrumented code (an assembly excluded from rewriting, or the framework) registers on a task | Follow the BCL's dispatch ([documented holes](#documented-holes-not-rewritten-in-these-rule-sets)). |
| Everything else | Runs as a BCL call. Work it starts on another thread fails the run at its first call to a controlled API, which throws `SimulationThreadAffinityException` ([thread affinity](compatibility.md#thread-affinity)); work which only computes or changes shared state runs unchecked, racing the logical thread. |

## Audited framework assemblies

| Assembly | Reason | Behavior under simulation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Microsoft.Extensions.Caching.Abstractions` | `FrameworkAwait` | `HybridCache` and the `IDistributedCache` extensions await the cache and the factory they are given. |
| `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting.Abstractions` | `FrameworkAwait` | `BackgroundService.StopAsync` awaits the service's work. The `IHost` extensions, which await the host's work in framework code, block on it, or bound a stop with a real timer, are controlled (`Hosting` family), and `BackgroundService.StartAsync`, which starts the service's work with the real `Task.Run`, is rejected (`BackgroundWork` family). |
| `Microsoft.Extensions.Options` | `FrameworkAwait` | Asynchronous options validation awaits the validators it is given. |
| `System.ComponentModel.Annotations` | `FrameworkAwait` | Asynchronous validation awaits the validation attributes it is given. |
| `System.Data.Common` | `FrameworkAwait` | `DbDataSource` awaits the provider's connections and commands. |
| `System.Formats.Tar` | `FrameworkAwait` | `TarReader` and `TarWriter` await the stream they are given; `TarFile` also reads and writes the file system. |
| `System.IO.Compression` | `FrameworkAwait` | The compression streams and `ZipArchive` await the stream they wrap. |
| `System.IO.Compression.Brotli` | `FrameworkAwait` | `BrotliStream` awaits the stream it wraps. |
| `System.IO.Compression.ZipFile` | `OsResource` | `ZipFile` and its extensions read and write archives and entries on the file system. |
| `System.IO.FileSystem.Watcher` | `AffinityBackstop` | `FileSystemWatcher` raises its events as the OS reports changes, on a thread of its own (Linux, macOS) or the thread pool (Windows). |
| `System.IO.Pipelines` | `FrameworkAwait` | `PipeReader` and `PipeWriter` copy to and from the stream or pipe they are given, awaiting it. |
| `System.IO.Pipes` | `OsResource` | Named and anonymous pipes are OS handles; `NamedPipeClientStream.ConnectAsync` connects on the thread pool. |
| `System.Linq.AsyncEnumerable` | `FrameworkAwait` | The operators await the sources and the asynchronous selectors and predicates they are given. |
| `System.Memory` | `FrameworkAwait` | The stream over a `ReadOnlySequence<byte>` awaits the destination it copies to. |
| `System.Net.Http` | `FrameworkAwait` | The content types await the streams they are given and the handlers await the handler they wrap; the default handler performs network I/O. `HttpClient`'s sending members, which bound each request with `HttpClient.Timeout` on a real timer, are controlled (`HttpClient` family). `MessageProcessingHandler` is listed by member. |
| `System.Net.Http.Json` | `FrameworkAwait` | The `HttpContent` extensions await the content they are given. The `HttpClient` extensions, which bound the request and the reading of its response with `HttpClient.Timeout` on a real timer, are controlled (`HttpClient` family). |
| `System.Net.HttpListener` | `OsResource` | `HttpListener` serves requests from the OS's HTTP stack or its own sockets. |
| `System.Net.Mail` | `OsResource` | `SmtpClient` sends over a network connection and raises `SendCompleted` through the captured `SynchronizationContext` or on the thread pool. |
| `System.Net.NameResolution` | `OsResource` | `Dns` resolves names through the OS resolver on the thread pool. |
| `System.Net.NetworkInformation` | `AffinityBackstop` | `NetworkChange` raises its events as the OS reports address and availability changes, on a thread of the BCL's choosing. |
| `System.Net.Ping` | `OsResource` | `Ping` sends ICMP echo requests through the OS and raises `PingCompleted` through the captured `SynchronizationContext` or on the thread pool. |
| `System.Net.Quic` | `OsResource` | QUIC connections, listeners, and streams run over the OS's QUIC implementation. |
| `System.Net.Requests` | `OsResource` | `WebRequest` and its HTTP, FTP, and file implementations perform network and file I/O. |
| `System.Net.Security` | `FrameworkAwait` | `SslStream` and `NegotiateStream` await the stream they wrap; their handshakes use the OS's security libraries. |
| `System.Net.ServerSentEvents` | `FrameworkAwait` | `SseParser` and `SseFormatter` await the stream and the items they are given. |
| `System.Net.Sockets` | `OsResource` | Sockets, `TcpClient`, `TcpListener`, and `UdpClient` are OS sockets, whose operations complete on the OS's I/O threads or the thread pool. |
| `System.Net.WebClient` | `OsResource` | `WebClient` performs network I/O and raises its completion events through the captured `SynchronizationContext` or on the thread pool. |
| `System.Net.WebSockets` | `FrameworkAwait` | `WebSocketStream` and the `WebSocket` base members await the `WebSocket` they are given. The `WebSocketStream.Create` overloads which make a stream own its socket, whose disposal bounds the close with a real timer, are controlled, and the factories which run the framework's protocol over a caller's stream are rejected (`WebSockets` family). |
| `System.Net.WebSockets.Client` | `OsResource` | `ClientWebSocket` connects over the network. |
| `System.Private.Xml` | `FrameworkAwait` | The asynchronous `XmlReader` and `XmlWriter` members await the stream, reader, or writer they wrap; `XmlUrlResolver` opens files and network resources. |
| `System.Private.Xml.Linq` | `FrameworkAwait` | `XDocument`, `XElement`, and `XNode` load and save through an asynchronous `XmlReader` or `XmlWriter`. |
| `System.Security.Cryptography` | `FrameworkAwait` | `CryptoStream` and the asynchronous hashing members await the stream they are given. |
| `System.Text.Json` | `FrameworkAwait` | The serializer, `JsonDocument`, `JsonNode`, and `Utf8JsonWriter` await the stream or asynchronous sequence they are given. |
| `System.Text.RegularExpressions` | `WaitsForFrameworkWork` | Parsing a deeply nested pattern continues on a fresh stack on the thread pool while the constructor waits. |
| `System.Transactions.Local` | `AffinityBackstop` | A transaction's timeout runs on a real timer, and when it expires the abort and the enlistments' notifications run on the thread pool; distributed transactions use the OS's transaction coordinator. |

# Documented holes (not rewritten in these rule sets)

These nondeterministic or entropy-drawing surfaces are intentionally **not** covered and
remain real BCL calls even under simulation:

- Every member listed above with the `CultureOrTimeZone`, `HostState`, `Process`, `RealClock`, `ProcessWideId`, `CurrentThread`, `IdentityHandle`, `ConstructorCall`, `Blocks`, `Schedules`, `ParallelQuery`, `FrameworkWrapper`, `DataflowBlock`, `BackgroundWork`, or `WebSocket` reason.
- A delegate created from a member, such as `Func<bool> responds = process.CloseMainWindow`, calls the BCL member: the rules rewrite `call`, `callvirt`, and `newobj` sites, and a delegate is created with `ldftn` or `ldvirtftn`.
- Blocking `ValueTask<T>.Result` reads: the accessor is not rewritten, so it would block the logical thread on an incomplete value task. `await`, or `GetAwaiter().GetResult()` through the controlled awaiter (which pumps until the value task completes), is the supported controlled path.
- Continuations registered by uninstrumented code (an assembly excluded from rewriting, or the framework itself) follow the BCL's dispatch, so a source which runs its continuations asynchronously runs them on the thread pool.
- The framework members listed under [Concurrency entry points: status](#concurrency-entry-points-status) with a reason, and any work a BCL call starts on another thread (the table's last row).
- Named/cross-process synchronization (named `EventWaitHandle`/`Mutex`/`Semaphore` and their `OpenExisting`/`TryOpenExisting` APIs): a single-process simulation cannot model kernel-object sharing, so these are rejected.
- Custom `TimeProvider` implementations are rejected by timer-consuming controlled APIs unless Clockwork explicitly recognizes them. `System.Timers.Timer` rejects non-null `SynchronizingObject` and designer `Site` integration because those paths can marshal callbacks to uncontrolled UI or native threads. `Timer.Dispose(WaitHandle)` accepts controlled event handles only.

Determinism is claimed **only** for the exact rules tabulated above.
